From then, for the rest of the turn (on the Radiant face, a Field Trap, for as long as it is on the field), a heal on an enemy — the enemy hero or an enemy unit; Lifesteal, "heal up to" and "heal to full" are heals, setting health is not — deals that much Pierce damage to it instead, from Blood Moon (§6.3 Heal). The Radiant face's type is its own (§5.2), so making the card Radiant in hand turns it into a Field Trap there | C+ #22; §5.2, §6.2, §6.3 |
| R414 | C+ #25 Soul Shot | Lucky needs a "best" (§6.1: a comparator per effect). For C+ #25's random enemy Unit to destroy, the better of the two picks is the Unit with the higher attack plus current health, then the higher cost, then the lower lane | C+ #25; §6.1, R32 |
| R415 | C+ #26 Tommy Tempo | C+ #26's "one more action" is one main-phase action — a play, an attack, a position switch or an activation — and ending the turn by hand also uses it; once it resolves the turn ends (§6.3 End the turn). The base face ends its controller's turn when it is cast on draw on their turn, the rest of the effect list resolving first, so drawn at the start of their turn it ends that turn before the main phase; cast on the opponent's turn, there is no turn of its controller's to end and only the summon happens | C+ #26; §6.3, R70 |
| R416 | C+ #27 Zephrys Zealotism | C+ #27 keeps the hand's size ("replace"), and "only Classic & Classic+" covers both faces, as #97's "only from the core set" does: R29's scorer ranks every non-token Classic and Classic+ card but this one for the state now (R387), each other card in its caster's hand goes to their graveyard (#76's reading), and as many new cards arrive, the scorer's top distinct cards (Radiant on the Radiant face); then its caster's mana is Refreshed (R364), which gives back the 4 this cost. With no other card in hand it gives nothing but the Refresh | C+ #27, #76, #97; R29, R364, R387 |
| R417 | C+ #29 Portal to the Past | C+ #29's "your last game" is its caster's last finished game of the same kind — server matches for a server match, practice games for practice; a hotseat game has none, and the card fizzles. That game's board is every permanent on the field when it ended, on both sides, except the opponent's face-down cards, which the caster never saw, each as its card and face only: stats, buffs and damage are not carried, a fused card is kept by its id (R179), and a token may come back as a card in hand (R11). Each seat's last board is an input of `createGame` beside its deck and handicap and is frozen into the match, so the match still folds exactly from its inputs and log (§9.3); the server keeps it per profile and practice on the device. The base face Discovers among 3 different entries; the Radiant face adds 3 different random ones; each costs (0) | C+ #29; §9.3, R11, R179 |
| R418 | C+ #33 Ivory Tower (a backrow pile a Unit may top) | C+ #33's aura gives its controller's cards Stack, so backrow cards may pile on backrow cards (a backrow pile, §3.2), and a Unit its controller plays may name the Tower's zone and top its pile. That Unit is a Unit for every rule, not a backrow card — it acts, is targeted and hit by "all Units" effects, and can't attack or be attacked (on the Radiant face it also becomes Radiant) — so backrow effects pass it by. The Tower stays active beneath the Unit it carries, the one exception to "only the top of a pile acts", as R13 makes Felinor Fiender's count one: its aura and its protection keep working, and "all Field Spells" effects still find it. It carries one Unit. If the Tower leaves the field, the Unit moves to its controller's unit zone in that lane, or the leftmost open one (R64), without leaving the field (no Cry and no reset, R78), and with no open unit zone it is destroyed | C+ #33; §3.2, R13, R64, R78 |
| R419 | C+ #35 Rollback | At the start of every turn, before anything else happens, the state keeps a snapshot of the field — both sides' piles and backrow zones as whole instances, and their Locks — and holds the last four (§10.1). C+ #35's "N turns ago" is the snapshot taken at the start of the player-turn N before this one, and its Radiant face also chooses a side or both. Restoring a side: first each card on it now that the snapshot does not hold leaves the field for its owner's hand (a token ceases to exist, R11); then each card the snapshot holds is put back in its zone as it was (damage, buffs, position, counters, controller), moved from wherever it is now or recreated from the snapshot if it no longer exists (a token, a card transformed or fused away); then that side's Locks become the snapshot's. Nothing else changes: health, mana, decks and graveyards stay, but for the cards that moved. No Cry and no Death fire, and one `rolledBack` event reports it beside the moves | C+ #35; §2.2, §10.1, R11, R78 |
| R420 | C+ #42 KY's Test | C+ #42 asks two prompts: the three difficulties, each labelled with the reward rolled for it as the Spell resolves (one random entry of its list), then the chosen problem, its statement and four options in an rng-shuffled order, from a question bank that is data in the cards package, public like the catalog, with at least 30 problems per difficulty (the Easy ones generated from the rng). The engine checks the answer and the key never leaves it: the options go to the chooser, the key stays in the prompt's resume data, which the view never sends (§10.8), and the opponent sees only that a prompt is open, then the reward's ordinary events. A wrong answer gives nothing, the Spell still played. Statements are plain text with Unicode maths, so a client needs no maths renderer; the turn clock runs while a player works, as for any prompt (R79). "3 Coins" are three The Coin tokens (§7), and "fill your hand" adds random Books until the hand holds 10 (R4) | C+ #42, C+ #42.1; §7, §10.6, §10.8, R4, R79 |
| R421 | C+ #60 Doctors Orders | C+ #60 is a Field Spell (the designer wrote "Unit, Spell" and gave no stats), since "Cry and start of turn" is how the Classic+ set writes one (C+ #7 The House; C+ #31 Fusion Lab's "Cry and end of turn"), as §5.3 settled #68's "Spell, Unit": its Cry and each start of its controller's turn add an All Purpose Apple to their hand (a Radiant one on the Radiant face) | C+ #60, C+ #7, C+ #31, C+ #59, #68; §5.3 |
| R422 | C+ #62 KY's Papaya | C+ #62's player picks points, not coefficients: its curve over the board — x the lane (0 to 4 from its caster's lane 1), y the row (0 their backrow, 1 their units, 2 the enemy units, 3 the enemy backrow) — is the lowest-degree polynomial, at most cubic, through the 1 to 4 cells the caster picks in different lanes, in exact rationals (Lagrange), which is how a player "creates" one without typing it. The cells are asked one prompt at a time (a board-cell prompt, §10.6), each offering the 4 cells of every lane not yet used and, after the first, "done", so every curve stays reachable within the prompt answer bound. A card is on the curve when its cell satisfies y = p(x) exactly — cards are points at their cells, and between lanes the curve touches nothing — so four cells fix the curve, which may also cross the fifth lane. Every card on it is exiled (on the Radiant face, every enemy card), and a client may show the equation | C+ #62; §10.6 |
| R423 | Call to Chaos Radiant: three different random effects | The Radiant face of both Call to Chaos cards, #95 and C+ #73, casts three different random effects from its own list of ten, resolved in the list's order with the recursion ("cast a random Call to Chaos") where it falls, in place of #95's old Radiant pair of the recursion and one other, so one rule serves both editions (R28, R87). The three are different entries (R60), each resolving as the base face's one does, and a recursion drawn at `CALL_TO_CHAOS_CHAIN_CAP` resolves into nothing and is not replaced (R87) | #95, C+ #73; R28, R60, R87, R436 |
| R424 | C+ #73.1 Classic Golem | C+ #73.1 transforms after the combat of an attack it declared on a Unit, so its own stats fight: it becomes a random non-token Unit of Classic or Classic+ (a Transform on the field keeps the type, as R35's does, §6.3), and if the Golem was the defender's killer (R42) the new Unit may attack again this turn, with a fresh exertion and not summoning sick. "May attack again" passes to the new Unit, the one reading in which both of the Golem's lines can matter | C+ #73.1; §6.3, R35, R42 |
| R425 | C+ #74 Twice Forward One Step Backwards | C+ #74 is a Field Trap (the designer wrote Trap), since a Trap is consumed when it fires, which would leave nothing to gain Brittle. Its printed Brittle starts when it is set (R385). It counts the opponent's plays from then on, and on each even count, after that card resolves, the card — if it still exists: a Unit on the field, a Spell in the graveyard, a trap in the backrow — is fused into this (R77: this is the kept instance and stays a Field Trap, and the opponent's card ceases to exist; on the Radiant face a Radiant copy of it is fused in instead), and then this gains +1 Brittle. The texts fused in work for its controller where they can (an end-of-turn line runs on their side, an aura covers it), a fused Cry never runs, since this is already on the field, and it turns face-up the first time it fuses (R33) | C+ #74; R33, R77, R385 |
| R426 | Prem Panther draws only after it attacks and survives | #32 draws for its attacks and nothing else: once a combat it attacked in — declared or forced (R53) — is over and the state check after it has run (R59), if it is still on the field on the stay it attacked from (R174), the player who controlled it in that combat draws 2 for each Unit that attack destroyed (R42, R212): the attacked Unit and, on the Radiant face, each Unit its Cleave killed. It never draws while defending, and never when it dies in the combat, even one Reborn brings back. The issue asked for it: under R42 alone it drew when it traded itself away and when it killed an attacker | #32, #86; R42, R53, R59, R174, R212 |
| R427 | Sheepish fires after the Cry | #41 answers the opponent's play of a Unit after that Unit resolves, its Cry included, and then transforms it into a Sheep Token; the Radiant face still adds a Lava Golem that costs (0) to its controller's hand. So a Unit no longer loses its Cry to it, and a counter, which answers the play's announce before `cardPlayed` (§10.5), always comes first. It still fires and is consumed when the Unit is Immutable (R23) or has left the field in its own resolution (R174), its Transform finding nothing and the Radiant face's "Also" still adding the Golem (R120). It rewrites R17's Sheepish half | #41; §10.5, R17, R23, R120, R174 |
| R428 | Carnivorous Cube eats only Units | #22's Cry tributes one of its controller's other Units — the top of a unit pile, an animated card included (R383) — and never a backrow card: it must eat one if it can, cannot eat itself, and with no other Unit the Cry fizzles and its Death does nothing. The issue asked for it, and R41's backrow meals are gone | #22; R41, R383 |
| R429 | KY's Math Equation counts its plays; its cost stops at (4) | #31 deals Fib(times played + 1) damage to a target (Fib(times played + 3) on the Radiant face) and at the end of the turn returns to its owner's hand with its cost 1 higher, never above (4). Times played counts this card's plays, the current one included, on the instance (§10.1), kept in every zone and through leaving the field, like `costMod` (R78): so its 1st play deals Fib(2) = 1, its 2nd Fib(3) = 2, its 3rd 3, its 4th 5, its 5th 8, and the index still clamps at 11 (R25). A cast is a play (R70) and counts; a countered play is never played (§10.5) and does not; a copy or a Transform is a new card with a count of its own (R57). Its cost plays no part in the damage (R67). The return raises its own cost (R65: printed plus `costMod`) by 1 but never above (4), so at (4) it adds nothing | #31; §10.1, §10.5, R25, R57, R65, R67, R70, R78 |
| R430 | Bear Honeypot waits while its controller's unit row is full | #60 stays face-down — it does not fire and is not consumed — while its controller has no empty, unlocked, unreserved unit zone (R64, R383); that is part of its condition (R99), so it waits for a later play once a zone opens, and with one zone open it fires and summons what fits (R64). The issue asked for it: it used to fire, and be spent, into a full board | #60; R17, R64, R99, R383 |
| R431 | Hinder's base face discards 1, the caster's choice | #21's base face reads "Cast on draw: Your opponent has 1 less mana next turn. Discard 1." (the opponent's next mana refresh is 1 lower): its caster discards one card of their choice (R16), a hand prompt that pauses the draw (R158), and with an empty hand discards nothing. The Radiant face is unchanged, "Cast on draw: Your opponent has 2 less mana next turn.", with no discard | #21; R16, R158 |
| R432 | "(N) Cost" is the noun, "costs (N)" the verb | Card text, and every client string a player reads, writes a specific cost as the noun "(N) Cost" ("a (1) Cost or less card", "(4)+ Cost cards", "Discover a (2) Cost card", "Face-down trap, (2) Cost") and a price as the verb "costs (N)" ("costs (1) less", "costs (0)"), never "Cost (N)", "N-cost" or "costing N", as issue #40 asked; it replaces R366's "Cost (N)" and R370's label, and the text pass rewrites every Core text with it. Players still read Deck and Tribute (R373). It is presentation: no rule reads it (CLAUDE.md rule 7) | Every card; §8, §10.10, R366, R370, R373 |
| R433 | A dealt deck lists only the cards its owner has been shown | A deck its player was dealt rather than built — All Random's (R258) and practice's fresh random deck — is no longer listed in full from the start, as R310 had it: `createGame` learns which seats were dealt a deck, and for those no record is written for the starting deck (R311), so the view's list of the player's own library counts each such card as unknown, and a client draws it as a card back, until it leaves the library (R312). What goes in openly afterwards — a mulligan's returns, a shuffle-in both players watched — is recorded and listed as R311 says, so the list shows only what its owner has been shown going in. A deck its player built is listed from the start as before. It is a view rule; the client draws what the view gives (CLAUDE.md rule 7) | §9.9, §10.8, R258, R310, R311, R312 |
| R434 | The game's end reveals both hands | Once the game is over, each player's view carries the other player's hand face-up, the cards as they stand (§10.8), and a client shows the opponent's hand with the result. It reveals the hands and nothing else: libraries and their order and face-down traps stay hidden, and R97 still judges the events, so an event that named a card while it was hidden stays redacted. The game is decided, so nothing a hand held can change it. It is a view rule, drawn by the client (CLAUDE.md rule 7) | §2.5, §10.8, R97 |
| R435 | Effects speed runs 0.25× to 3× | The viewer's effects speed is a slider from `FX_SPEED_MIN` 0.25 to `FX_SPEED_MAX` 3, as issue #40 asked, in place of R201's 0.5 to 2; everything else R201 says stands. It is a per-viewer presentation setting: nothing in the engine, the view or the event stream changes | §10.10, R201 |
| R436 | Call to Chaos names the effects it rolled, to both players | Each effect #95 or C+ #73 rolls is named in the event stream to both players, unredacted — which entry of its list it is — before that effect resolves, and a client shows the rolled effect, or the three, on the card as it resolves, so it is plain which were chosen. The play is public and so is what it does, so naming the roll hides nothing it ought to keep. It is a view rule, drawn by the client (CLAUDE.md rule 7) | #95, C+ #73; §10.3, §10.8, R97, R423 |
| R437 | A marked card shows its mark: K-Pop Fanatic's pending steal on its target | A card an effect has marked for something still to come carries that mark in both players' views while it waits: #50 K-Pop Fanatic's pending steal (R76) marks its target from the Cry until the steal resolves or fizzles, and a client draws the mark on the card as a corruption sparkle in the mark's colour (purple for #50), one effect reusable in other colours for other marks. The target was chosen in a public play, so the mark tells neither player anything the play did not; on a face-down target the other player's back carries it (R33). It is a view rule, drawn by the client (CLAUDE.md rule 7) | #50; §10.8, R33, R76, R97 |
| R438 | Keyword visuals on the board | The board draws the keywords a unit has — Taunt, Divine Shield and the others the view carries for it (§10.4's computed set) — as visuals on the unit itself, not only in its text, so a player reads them at a glance on both seats. Each is drawn from the view alone and holds still under the reduce setting and `prefers-reduced-motion` (R200); none is a rule a client enforces (CLAUDE.md rule 7) | §6.1, §10.4, §10.10, R200 |
| R439 | The last 30 seconds of a turn clock | When the active player's turn clock (R79) has 30 seconds or less left, the board shows a visual timer effect for it, read off the deadline the server sends and still under reduced motion (R200). It is presentation: the server keeps the clock, and nothing changes in the game when the effect starts (CLAUDE.md rule 7) | §10.10, R79, R200 |
| R440 | Hidden piles reveal no counts | A change made to cards someone may not read (a hand, a deck, a face-down trap) never tells the other player how many cards a filter let through. A Degrade, Upgrade or KY's Constant reports every application on such a card, with change `none` where nothing changed (Immutable, nothing applicable, or left out by the scope's filters). A scope or random pick reaches every hidden card of its piles whatever its filters say, and its events go out in R242's group order. A Brittle tick, and a buff, keyword, count or enchantment given over a scope, is reported only on cards both players read; the rest change silently and their owners read the change off the view. A hidden card's Brittle count never appears in an event. | C+ #8, C+ #23, C+ #40, C+ #70, C+ #73, C+ #74, T-AI-10 |
| R441 | Brittle edge cases | A count that has crumbled its card is spent: R78's and R215's reset drop a count of 0, and a card that re-enters the field starts its printed Brittle afresh. "Gain +N" on a card with no count starts one now at N more than it prints; that count is a given one, so a Vanilla keeps it. A card dormant under a Stack does not tick (R13), and no card in a hand or a deck does (R638). | C+ #23, C+ #74, T-AI-3 |
| R442 | How a Degrade or Upgrade draws | One application first draws the row uniformly among cost, stats, keyword, X and number rows that can change the card, then the item within the row, then the stats split k; a choice of one draws nothing (R129). A Degrade removes only a keyword the card has of its own (printed, Upgrade-added or granted; every entry of that kind), never one an aura or Defense Position lends. Upgrade-added keywords are card text: a Vanilla hides them, and nothing is added to a Vanilla unit. An X-cost card with no chosen X always takes its X step. Immutable refuses KY's Constant, and its "random" picks among numbers not already at the value. | C+ #8, C+ #41, C+ #69–C+ #73, T-AI-10 |
| R443 | Enchantments on copies and fusions | A copy, on the field or shuffled into a deck, carries its source's enchantments with its tuning. A Fuse unites its ingredients' enchantments, each once. A Transform makes a card with neither. The same enchantment is never on a card twice; two of one kind that differ are both kept. | C+ #14, C+ #40 |
| R444 | Flicker and unit tokens | A Flickered card leaves the field (R174) and re-enters its zone in the same place, on the same side: R78's reset, summoning sick, Attack Position, no Cry, no Death, events flickered then summoned; a unit token comes back like any card, as R175 brings one back through Reborn; a Trap re-enters face-down under a fresh id (R227); an animated card stays a Unit. | C #14 |
| R445 | Animating is not summoning | The card was on the field already, so animating emits animated, never summoned, and nothing answering a summon (C #5 Tesla) answers it | C #5, C #38, C+ #12.8 |
| R446 | A carried Unit | A Unit on a carrier stands in its backrow zone, is a Unit for every rule, can neither attack nor be attacked (forced included) and binds no attacker with Taunt, may switch position; the zone takes nothing more; when the zone's acting card stops carrying (left the field, or Vanilla'd) the Unit steps down at the next state check to its lane's unit zone, else the leftmost open one, without leaving the field, not summoning sick (animated with carried); with none it is destroyed once, and an Indestructible one waits for a zone; a rotation or swap carries it with its carrier | C+ #33 |
| R447 | Backrow piles | A Stack card (keywords where it stands, hand auras and grants included) tops an occupied backrow zone unless it is Locked, held, or carrying a Unit; only the top acts (a trap beneath never fires, an aura beneath is off, a buried Heroic Power can't be used); both seats see a count, never an identity, and the AI's redaction hides a face-down card beneath (R185); a resumed card answers nothing of its uncovering (R212); whole-board moves carry the pile whole | C+ #33, C+ #77, C #51 |
| R448 | The announce window | The paid-for card waits in its player's resolving zone (out of every hand effect's reach) and `cardAnnounced` goes out. Traps answer first, then the triggers the announce woke, each followed by the state check. The first Counter sends the card to its owner's graveyard, to exile, or to the countering player's hand as theirs (their hand cap applies). Later traps stay set and later triggers fizzle. A countered play is never counted, has no Cry, `cardPlayed`, `cardResolved` or Echo, and its mana, Tributes and discards stay spent. A card being set face-down is its player's alone: the opponent sees its zone and cost, and the type "Trap" | C #4, C #10, C #17, C #72, C #87; T-AI-9 |
| R449 | Step 3's v0.2.0 rules | A live `replacePlays` replaces each play or cast by a new card. The old card ceases to exist, the price paid stays, the new card's choices are asked before the announce, and it takes its zone by R64. A card already identical to the named one is not replaced. A tag rule makes every play carrying that tag Radiant, read at step 1 as R214 reads Gifted Program | C #23; C+ #68 |
| R450 | The targeting point | Targeting means a declared `target` pick or a `target` prompt answer. A costly target carries its discards, or its prompt asks for them next. It is not a legal target with too few other cards in hand, and the cost binds both players. A Spell's declarations and prompts never offer a card Immune to Spells. A hand interceptor is summoned (no Cry, summoning sick) and the first pick moves to it if it fits that declaration; a cost already paid stays paid | C #33, C #89; C+ #19.1 |
| R451 | Play records | Plays by type this turn, plays by tag this game, the last Spell (a copy records what it copied) and each player's last face-up card (not Traps or AI cards). Casts count; countered plays never | C #57; C+ #37, C+ #64; T-AI-2, T-AI-5 |
| R452 | Random casts | A random cast (C+ #47, C+ #38.1) makes every choice its caster would make at random, uniformly: declared targets and modes, Echo repeats, and every prompt opened for its caster while it resolves (Discover, its text, nested casts), answered at once, so the caster is never asked; a cast made inside it is random too. The other player's prompts are theirs and asked as usual. Its X is the caster's mana, at least 1. "Target enemies when possible" (and the `targetEnemies` enchantment, §10.1) narrows each target pick, random or chosen, and the card's own prompts, to the enemies among the options when there are enough. A random cast and the casts inside it make at most RANDOM_CAST_CHAIN_CAP (20) casts; one past that resolves into nothing | C+ #38.1, C+ #40, C+ #47 |
| R453 | Casts from anywhere | Cast a card that exists (hand, deck, graveyard, exile, never the field; it leaves its pile as the cast begins and lands in its owner's piles), or a new card of a named definition (caster-owned, to the caster's graveyard, R87). A non-random cast X card asks its caster for X (1..mana, at least 1) before its targets and modes, and pays nothing. "Then exile them" exiles the resolved Spell at step 7. A cast Field Spell or Trap is placed (a Trap face-down); with no zone it fizzles: still played, nothing resolves, lands in the graveyard. A cast's choices come after step 3 and before the announce | C #7, C #47, C #56, C+ #37 |
| R454 | Playing from the graveyard | While a card on its controller's side grants it (every type, or Units only; an optional least price as it would be paid; or paid with that card's Plague Tokens), `legalActions` offers a play of a card in the player's own graveyard, taken out at step 4, with choices, counting, Cry and R65's prices as from hand; such a card is priced as a play wherever it is read. A Plague payment spends at least MIN_PLAGUE_PAYMENT token, at most the tokens there and the price, the rest in mana; `costPaid` is the whole price; a hand play spends none. A card gone from the graveyard by step 4 is not played (R226) | C #28, C #74, C #90 |
| R455 | Cost rules | After R65's discounts: flat price rules (modifiers and auras), then threshold rules (each reads the number the flat rules left, as R363 reads Curvature), then "costs (N)", which wins over every add, then the card's floor, then 0. X cards ignore every rule but the floor and a ban. A rule "until used" is spent by the next play it applies to, whether or not it moved that play's price (a Trap C #6 already sets to (0) spends C #2's), never by a cast. A ban reads the final price and refuses plays from hand and graveyard, never a cast. Forever&'s rider is taken at step 4 by the next Spell played or cast; that Spell returns to hand from where it landed | C #2, C #6, C #68, C #77, C+ #14, T-AI-7 |
| R456 | End the turn from an effect | The rest of the list and of the action resolve and any prompt it opened is answered; then `turnCutShort` and every end-of-turn step. "N more actions" counts the player's main-phase actions (play, attack, switch, activation); the action that set it, answers and draw offers do not count. The turn ends once the Nth has resolved; ending the turn yourself uses it up; two riders keep the sooner end. Nothing happens off-turn. | C+ #26, T-AI-8 |
| R457 | Draw counts and limits | Every draw that happens counts on its turn for its player, whoever's turn it is: fatigue and named draws included, setup excluded. `drawn.turnDraw` is public. Field cards set limits; the lowest holds; a draw past it does nothing at all and emits a public `drawLimited`. | C #4, C #9, C #49, T-AI-4 |
| R458 | Next-turn and rest-of-game effects | A delayed destroy of a unit is keyed to its stay on the field (R174) and Indestructible ignores it; a scope reads the Units standing then. "This turn" is the running turn; "your next turn" skips the current one. A rest-of-game effect is the player's own (no `self`), runs among the delayed effects by `seq`, once per turn, and stacks. | C #20, C #37, C+ #52 |
| R459 | Cast on draw | The `castOnDraw` enchantment casts the drawn card; a Unit with no open unit zone goes to hand uncast. | C+ #26, C+ #40 |
| R460 | Several replacements of one event | They apply one at a time in R68 order; each card replaces an event at most once; each later one re-checks the changed event, so a second Final Gambit stays set | C #50, C #60, C #28, C #52 |
| R461 | Exiled instead of dying | Not a death: no Death, no Reborn, no destroyed event or count | C #50 |
| R462 | What a replacement moment is | "Heal X" is X even on an undamaged unit; heal to full or up to is what it would restore (0 is no heal). Would-die is §4.5 step 1's collection only, never a Sacrifice. A unit token never reaches a graveyard (R11). A follow-up is owed work resolving after the effect that replaced, before queued triggers | C #14, C #50, C #52, C+ #22 |
| R463 | Leaving together; hidden texts | Collected cards all leave the field before any lands, so a Voidwalker dying with them exiles none. Several divisors multiply, rounded up once. A face-down Trap guards no hero and converts no heal by its text | C #50, C #75, C+ #11, C+ #22 |
| R464 | Deck triggers in R68's order | A library card answers only its deck triggers. Within a side they come after hand triggers and before graveyard triggers, in instance creation order, never library position (§9.1). A library card's queue entry takes no number (R177). | C+ #37 |
| R465 | An answer's key never leaves the engine | The key stays in the `answer` prompt's resume data. `viewFor` never sends it, and the AI's redaction (R185) strips it from its own prompt and from parked work, so the AI answers from what the prompt shows. Without the key, no answer is judged right. Options appear under letters in an rng-shuffled order. | C+ #42 |
| R466 | Cards between players' piles | A card taken from a hand, deck, stack or field becomes the taker's: owner and controller. `stolen` reads to whoever could read the card where it was taken (hand → its holder; face-down → its controller; face-up or public pile → both; library → nobody) or can read it where it is now. A card taken from the other player's library makes the rest of that library unknown to its owner (R312). A draw from the opponent's deck is the drawer's draw; an empty deck gives nothing and no fatigue. | C #9, C #58, C+ #12.3, every card that steals off the field (§6.3 Steal) |
| R467 | Trigger a Cry | Runs the Cry of a Unit on top of its pile or in a graveyard, for the triggering controller. Declared targets and modes are asked as prompts in declaration order: modes first when a target belongs to a mode; an unsatisfiable declaration is skipped; a Tribute's slots are left `none` and nothing is paid. On the field the Unit is "this"; from a graveyard "this" finds nothing and X reads 0. It is not a play: no `cardPlayed` and no counters. A Unit that leaves before its choices are made triggers nothing. The Cry's own tail runs before the triggering card's (R113). | C #54 |
| R468 | Bounded fused ids | A fused id spells out its ingredients only while that list is at most `FUSED_ID_CAP` (120) characters. Past that it is `t-<n>:#<16 hex>`, a pure 64-bit digest of the list, with no crypto. Every fused def keeps `ingredients: {defId, radiant?}[]`. A digest's scripts are rebuilt from it on entry to reduce, legalActions and viewFor. Self-exclusion from pools (R387) still covers every ingredient. | C+ #73, C+ #74; R179, R387 |
| R469 | A Radiant ingredient | An ingredient fused "as a Radiant card" gives its Radiant face, text and scripts to both fused forms. The readable id marks it `name*`. With no kept card and every ingredient Radiant, the result is Radiant. | C+ #31, C+ #43, C+ #74 |
| R470 | Fusing into a hand or deck card; "its cost doesn't change" | A Fuse may keep a hand or deck card, which stays where it is. Its type wins, and an Immutable card is never kept. Keeping the cost means: an existing costOverride stays; else its current printed cost becomes its costOverride; an X-cost or embiggen card keeps that printed form. costMod stays. The `fused` event is hidden as the card is. A refused random fusion draws no rng. | C+ #31, C+ #73, C #78 (Radiant) |
| R471 | Plague placements | Every token gain is a placement on a permanent on the field, never a dormant card. It is multiplied by the receiving card's multiplier (at least 1; a fused card's multipliers multiply). It emits `counterChanged` with `placed`, once per placement; a removal carries no `placed`. "Place N" is N target prompts in R68 order, repeats allowed, with the rest of the list parked (R113) and the state check after the whole effect (R59). Tokens are public: a face-down card shows its count on its back to both players. | C #27, C #39, C #42, C #53, C #61, C #63, C #70, C #76, C #90; C+ #3; #91 |
| R480 | Tokens named by their tag | The eight Pancake tokens and the ten AI generated cards reach play only as members of a pool a card names by its tag — "a random Pancake token" (C+ #12 The Mother Pancake, C+ #13 Mommy Barker), "a random AI generated card" (C+ #43 AI Slop, C+ #78 Claude's Datacenter) — never by name, so R279's rule that every token is named by some card counts a text that names that pool as naming each of its members. A card's `refs` still lists only the cards its texts name by name (R279, both ways), and The Coin, which a rule deals (R244), is now also named by C+ #42 KY's Test ("3 The Coins"). The five Grapes are named one by one in Two Grapes' and Vine of Grapes' odds, so they need no pool rule | §5, §7, R244, R279, C+ #12, C+ #12.1–C+ #12.8, C+ #13, C+ #42, C+ #43, C+ #65, C+ #66, C+ #78, T-AI-1–T-AI-10 |
| R481 | A catalog that grows is granted | Everyone owns every card (§9.1), and R111's launch grant rides only the pending → active transition, so an account already active when a patch adds cards would never own them. When `app.settings.catalog_version` changes — `db:seed-catalog` stamps it after writing every card row of the new version, in the same transaction — every active account is granted the launch quantity of each non-token card of the new version it holds no `launch` grant for, through `app.grant_launch_collection_all` and the ledger (reason `launch`, ref `launch:<version>`, migration 0015). It is idempotent: the same stamp again, or a round trip through another version, grants nothing twice; a pending account waits for its own activation; a card a later catalog drops is never taken back | §9.1, §9.4, R111, every Classic and Classic+ card |
| R482 | How a card writes its tunable numbers | R386 declares a card's tunable numbers as `params` and writes them `{key}`. Every number a Classic or Classic+ card's "Tunes:" lists (§8.6, §8.7) is one param `{ key, base, radiant, better, step, min, max? }`: `better` "up" when more is better for the card's controller (an item with no arrow is its effect's own strength, "up"); `step` as stated, else `PARAM_DEFAULT_STEP` from the base value (1 up to 5, 2 for 6–12, a quarter above); `min` as stated, else 1, or 0 for a number printed 0, and never so low that the effect stops (Argusland's divisor stops at 2); `max` only when stated, 100 for a percentage. The ten AI generated cards have no "Tunes:" and declare none: their numbers are printed, and Degrade has fewer options on them. The number is written in each face's text that shows it and nowhere as a literal: `{key}` for the number alone ("Deal {damage} damage."), `{key|singular|plural}` for a count and the words that agree with it ("Draw {draw|card|cards}." prints "Draw 1 card." and "Draw 2 cards."), and a sentence after a count that would need an agreeing pronoun says "Each …" ("Each costs (0)."), so a text reads right at every value a Degrade or an Upgrade can move it to; a Radiant-only number is written in the Radiant text alone, with the same value on both faces. A numbered keyword (Armor, Lucky, Brittle, Spell Damage) and an Echo, Tribute or Activate count is no param: R386's X change tunes those. `fillParams` (packages/shared) fills a face from its printed values or an instance's current ones, and R277's diff, R276's check and the text tests read a face through it | §5, R276, R277, R386, every Classic and Classic+ card with a Numbers line, T-AI-1–T-AI-10 |
| R500 | The glossary's Cry and Tribute rows are short reminders | Patch v0.2.0 asked for shorter Cry and Tribute reminder text. The keyword glossary the inspect views print beside a card copies SPEC §6's Rule column verbatim (R373 puts it into players' words), except two rows, which it writes as one short line each that is still true to the rule: Cry, "When you play this card or an effect casts it. Never when it enters play otherwise" (§6.2's ruling, which it already stated in place of the Rule column), and Tribute, "Playing this also costs X of your Units, which go to the graveyard" (§6.3's cost, without R41's backrow clause, which R428 removed). The glossary names the two rows as its short reminders, and a test pins their words, keeps each to one line of at most 16 words, and checks every other row against SPEC as before. Presentation only: no rule reads the glossary (CLAUDE.md rule 7) | §6.2, §6.3, §10.10, R373, R428 |
| R501 | The voice set renders on macOS or Windows, and may take 6 MiB | §10.11 pre-rendered every voice line with macOS `say` and capped the set at 3 MB for Core's 111 cards; Classic and Classic+ bring 206 more cards and tokens and 274 more lines. `gen-voice.mjs` has two backends, named per persona: macOS `say` (Core's personas, whose files and hashes are unchanged) and Windows SAPI (`System.Speech`, driven through `powershell.exe`, natively or from WSL) shaped by `ffmpeg` (silence trimmed, pitch shifted by the persona's semitones with the tempo kept, the persona's filter chain for character, peak-normalised) and encoded to the same mono AAC at 22050 Hz and about 32 kbps. A SAPI persona's hash covers its own fields (voice, rate, semitones, filter, text), and its lines take no per-card override. Generating renders only the stale keys whose backend the machine has and reports the others as needing theirs; `--check` stays OS-independent and gains `--catalog` to check against another catalog. The budget, `VOICE_BUDGET_BYTES`, is 6 MiB counted in 4 KiB blocks (the 429 files take about 4.8 MiB), and every line still stays within 4 s, flavour only, and speaks at R204's moments. A missing file still falls back to the browser's speech synthesis | every card; §10.11, R203, R204 |
| R502 | A cast on draw is shown on both seats | A card cast the moment it is drawn (§2.4, R58) is known in the redacted stream by its `cardPlayed` coming right after its own `drawn` (a `cardAnnounced` of the same card may stand between; the sentinel matches either id; a card set face-down matches by `formerId`, R227), never by its definition. On both seats, the drawer's own too, it bursts out of the drawer's Deck pile and the showcase holds it up under a "Cast on draw!" ribbon, longer than a play (`SHOWCASE_CAST_HOLD_MS` over the effects speed, at most `SHOWCASE_CAST_HOLD_CAP_MS`, under `PRACTICE_SHOWCASE_HOLD_MAX_MS`), as the runner reaches its `cardPlayed`; a hidden card is a back and is never named. `CARD_FX` gives a card its own recipe over its resolution: #21 Hinder cracks the crystals of the victim's tray that its next refresh will not fill, as many as the view's rider badge says (R169), and they stay frosted and cracked while the view lists the rider, drawn in every mode and still under reduced motion; #27 Blood Ridden Glowy Jelly Bean runs a crimson stream from the caster's hero into the card made Radiant, on the other seat into a back picked by the play's own event count, so it never says which card. It paces nothing (R200) | #21, #27; §2.4, §10.10, R58, R97, R169, R200, R202, R227 |
| R503 | Card art and set marks | Every face shows its set as a small mark on its frame: a ringed orb for Core, a columned temple for Classic, the temple with a plus for Classic+, and a plain diamond for a set the client has no glyph for. The mark is named by its title and label ("Classic+ set"), sits clear of the cost gem, name, rarity gem, rules box, stats, crest and Radiant marks, and is hidden only on faces under 100 px. A token that prints a rarity (§5's `printedRarity`) wears that rarity's frame, gem, crest and foil, on the board too; its rarity stays Token everywhere else. The procedural art gains the Book, Pancake and AI families (tag themes: AI after Call to Chaos, Book after KY, Pancake before Fruit, so a family's token reads as its family) and a motif read from each card's printed name. A plain card or Token wears its motif as its emblem; a tribe or family carries it beside its own emblem and skips the motif its own picture already draws. Every catalog entry draws a distinct picture on each face, and a Core card whose name calls up no motif draws exactly the picture it drew before. Presentation only: no rule reads the art, the mark or the printed rarity | every card; the tokens with a printed rarity (C+ #12.1–C+ #12.8, C+ #19.1–C+ #19.5, C+ #32.1–C+ #32.3, C+ #36.1, C+ #38.1, C+ #42.1, C+ #46.1, C+ #65.1–C+ #65.5, C+ #73.1, C+ #75.1, C+ #76.1) |
| R504 | An empty hand keeps its place | Patch v0.2.0 asked that empty hands not minimise during a battle. A hand with no cards, either seat's, keeps its row: the client draws one card-sized dashed outline worded "Hand empty" in it, sized in every layout (desktop, a tablet either way, a phone upright or on its side) exactly as that seat's cards are, so the row keeps a card's height and the field does not move when the last card leaves or the first arrives. The outline is not a card: nothing plays, drags or targets it, and the hand count reads 0. Presentation only (CLAUDE.md rule 7) | §10.8, §10.10 |
| R505 | The Find a match box repeats no queue count | Patch v0.2.0 asked to remove the queue counts from the Find a match box, which repeated the mode tiles above it. The lobby shows how many players are waiting (R257) once, on each mode's tile ("N waiting"). The box says only what the screen is doing (Find a match, Leave the queue, "Looking for a … opponent…"), and the notice after queueing names the queue joined, not its size. The room code is unchanged. Presentation only | §9.5, R257 |
| R506 | Card moments in sound | A sound may answer the readable play an event happens inside: the innermost `cardPlayed` whose `cardResolved` has not come, forgotten when a turn starts, never a play whose card is the sentinel. #21 Hinder's rider landing on its victim's next refresh (`modifierChanged` "nextTurnMana", whether it adds a rider or cancels one) is a glassy mana crack in place of the plain notice, on both seats, since that is where the victim's mana visibly changes. A card #27 Blood Ridden Glowy Jelly Bean turns Radiant is a blood drain and then a gold burst in place of the plain glint, the same on the opponent's seat, where the `radiantSet` carries the sentinel, because the sound reads the public cast and never the card it hit. A readable `drawn` directly followed by that card's `cardPlayed` is a card cast as it is drawn, and stings in place of the play whoosh, except a Trap's. A token's printed rarity (§5's `printedRarity`) gives its summon the Legendary or Mythic sting as a card's rarity does. Call to Chaos's roll (R436) spins like a slot machine and dings once for each effect it names, at most three. A mark (R437) brands its card as it lands and lets go softly as it lifts, whatever the mark, its colour or the card. Book, Pancake and AI are sound families like the other tags, following the card art's theme (R503). The last 30 seconds of the viewer's own running turn clock (R439) beat once a second through the effects bus, a heartbeat and then, in the last 10, a tick sharper each second; never the other seat's clock, and nothing while muted, at master or effects volume 0, or before the first gesture. None of this is a rule, and R203 bounds all of it | #21, #27, #50, #95, C+ #73, the Book, Pancake and AI cards, tokens with a printed rarity; §10.11, R202, R203, R436, R437, R439 |
| R507 | How the patch history marks and lists changes | Words and numbers a patch added to a face's text are marked bold, with a double underline, in a teal of their own, distinct from R277's gold and R279's dotted line; screen readers hear "new:". Where the patch took words away, the old text follows under "Was", with them struck through (read as "removed:"). Values read "(2) Cost → (1) Cost", "2/3 → 3/3". On the Patch notes page only the newest patch shows its cards on arrival; the others load their snapshot and the one before when opened. A patch's cards can be filtered by name. A changed card whose change shows on a face appears as one face from that patch's snapshot: the Radiant face if every printed change is the Radiant face's, else the base face, with its changes beside it. A card changed only in data no face prints (lines of code, cards named, tunable-number limits) is listed by name with what changed. Added cards are listed by name, grouped by set in catalog order, with each set's tokens as their own group; removed cards by name. A card opens as it stands now (the newest snapshot holding it, or the last before its removal) in the collection's detail view with its History open. Old faces in History mark referenced names but never make them controls. Every text is filled in (`fillParams`) before it is drawn or diffed, so no placeholder shows. Presentation only (CLAUDE.md rule 7) | §10.10, R105, R277, R279, R388, R432 |
| R508 | Practice's last board | A free practice game that ends gives the human the board R417 takes from it: the worker reads `lastBoardFor` of the final state from the human's seat, so the AI's face-down cards are left out, and sends it with the game's last snapshot. The page keeps that one board on the device, each finished game's replacing the one before; a read that fails or finds anything malformed is no board, and a write that fails keeps the old one. The next free practice game hands it to the human's seat as its `createGame` input, and the game's debug record keeps that input, so the game still folds from its inputs and log (§9.3). The AI's seat never has a last board: its board would name the face-down cards the human never saw, which the device must not hold, so the AI's C+ #29 fizzles. A tutorial lesson neither takes the board nor gives one (R291), and a game left before it ends gives none. Hotseat passes none (R417) | C+ #29; §9.3, §9.9, R187, R291, R417 |
| R510 | The Activate control | A card the viewer controls whose view lists `activations` wears one Activate control per ability on the card itself, since a click on the card stays its attack or its pick as a target: a lightning glyph and a badge with the uses left this turn ("∞" for Activate ♾️), and the ability's words, its numbers filled from the card's `params`, as its tooltip and accessible name. A control is live exactly when `legalActions` lists that card's `activate`; otherwise it is greyed, its tooltip gives the view's `reason`, and it sends nothing. A press builds the activation as a play's choices are built (R81): one listed activation is sent at once, and several wait for a target clicked on the board or dragged to from the control (or from a card of yours that has nothing to attack and one ability), a Tribute, or a mode in the inline picker. Heroic Power is built the same way from the hero panel, whichever of `activatePower` or `activate` is listed, and each power a player controls is its own control. The control flares while the `activated` row plays on its card, and under reduced motion shows a still ring instead. Presentation and intent only (CLAUDE.md rule 7) | Classic #7, #15, #20, #21, #23, #42, #78, #81, #84; Classic+ #76.1; Core #98; §6.2, §10.10, R43, R81, R384 |
| R511 | C #57 Echo's face shows the text it copies | Where the view gives a copier the Spell text it has (R399), its face prints that text, on the face it was played on and filled with the numbers the view gives it (R386), in place of its own copying sentence. Whatever else its face prints stays: a Radiant Echo reads "Echo 1" over the copied text. It keeps its own name, (1) Cost, type and art; its inspect notes say "Copying: <name>" ("(Radiant)" on a Radiant face) and its own printed text stands beside it. With no copy it prints its own text | C #57; R243, R386, R399, R546 |
| R512 | Glossary rows for patch v0.2.0's terms | The glossary has a row, in players' words, for every §6 term a v0.2.0 card prints: the §6.1 statuses Can't be in Defense Position (SPEC's "Cannot be…" an alias), Can't be attacked, Only Units in this lane can attack this and Berserk; §6.2's Activate (one term for "Activate", "Activate X" and "Activate ♾️"); §6.3's Counter, Steal, Unlock, Flicker, Plague Token, Set health, Redirect, End the turn, Trigger a Cry and Look at a hand, each matched by the words the cards print; and Degrade and Upgrade, which split the one "Degrade / Upgrade" row by pairing the words either side of its slash with the rest of its sentence. A rule is printed without the ruling numbers SPEC cites. Matching stays case-sensitive, so a lower-case "steal it" or "can't attack or be attacked" stays plain words. The glossary beside a face in play also explains the states it wears | §6, §10.10, R373, R500 |
| R513 | How a client marks a tuned card | A card the view reports changed by Degrade, Upgrade or KY's Constant is marked wherever the viewer may read it. Each change is better or worse for its controller: a stat, a numbered keyword or an X going up is better, except Tribute, which is better going down; a keyword added is better and one removed worse; a declared number follows its `better`; a value set outright that is not a declared number is neither. A moved number in the text sits in a dotted box with ▲ or ▼, a tuned stat carries the same glyph, an added keyword is a "+" chip and a removed one a struck "−" chip, and the card is marked Upgraded (▲, every change better), Degraded (▼, every change worse) or Tuned (◆). The inspect overlays list each change in words beside the printed text. A Brittle count shows as a cracked-glass badge with its number, an enchantment as a badge with its words, a backrow pile as its depth, and a card's lines of code in its detail line. Presentation only (CLAUDE.md rule 7) | Classic+ #8, #14, #23, #40, #41, #69–#74, T-AI-3, T-AI-10; §10.8, §10.10, R383, R385, R386 |
| R514 | Board cells answer a cell prompt | A `cell` prompt (Classic+ #62, one cell a prompt, R422) is answered on the board: the zones its options name glow green on both sides, filled or empty, as a play's zones do, and wear a crosshair while it is open; a click or a tap on one or on what stands in it, or Enter or Space on a focused one, sends that option. The picker lists the same cells, grouped by side and row and named by lane, with any option that names no cell ("Done") among them, for the keyboard and small screens. Which zones answer is read off the options alone; a zone not offered answers nothing. Presentation only (CLAUDE.md rule 7) | Classic+ #62; §10.6, R422 |
| R515 | A budgeted pick shows its total | A `pick` prompt with a budget (Classic #44's "total cost of (5) or less") draws each option as its card's face at the cost the option carries and keeps a running total in R432's words, "(3) of (5) spent"; every card not yet picked whose cost would take the total past the budget is greyed, says "Over the budget" and is not added when pressed, while a picked card can always be taken back. Confirm waits until the picks are within the prompt's `min` and `max` and the total within the budget. All of it is read off the prompt's `cost`s, `budget`, `min` and `max`; the engine still refuses an answer over the budget. Presentation only (CLAUDE.md rule 7) | Classic #34, #44; §10.6, R432 |
| R520 | C #7 InfiniScepter holding an X-cost Spell | An X-cost Spell costs (0) in a hand (R65), so C #7's Cry may exile one. Its Activate casts the copy with the X its caster chooses as the cast begins, from 1 to their current mana (R348), as a cast's other choices are its caster's (R70, R81); the cast pays nothing, so the X is not paid, and bounding it by current mana keeps the choice enumerable for `legalActions` and the prompt | C #7; R65, R70, R81, R348 |
| R521 | C #9 Income Tax: which draws count | The opponent's draws in a turn are every draw that happened that turn, whoever's turn it is: the start-of-turn draw, a draw an effect makes, a card burned on a full hand and a card cast on draw alike, since each left the deck by a draw (as R55's drawn counter counts them). A draw a draw limit stops never happened and does not count (§2.4). A draw from an empty deck happened and counts toward the number, but it draws no card, so it never sets the trap off itself. The trap fires on the draw that brings the count to exactly its number (2, tunable) when that draw draws a card, and on no other draw that turn: a fatigue draw that reaches the number sets nothing off, and the draws after it are past the number | C #9; §2.4, R55, R58 |
| R540 | C #90 reward J is next-turn mana | In Too Deep's reward J, "gain 100 mana", is next-turn mana: a +100 rider on its controller's next mana refresh (§2.3, R364's next-turn mana), not mana for the turn it is granted. Quest 7, the only quest that leads to J, completes as its controller's turn ends (end a turn with 5 or more unspent mana), so mana for that turn would lapse unused; the reward has its intended effect on the next turn. §8.6's row 90 reads "J, gain 100 mana" and is read this way | C #90; §2.3, R364, R404 |
| R541 | What counts as a draw for C #90 | Every draw of its controller that took a card from their deck counts toward quest 1 ("draw 2 cards"), whether the card was kept, burned at the hand cap (R4) or cast on draw (R58), as #100 Ceaseless Void's draw counter counts draws (R55, R225). A draw the draw limit stopped never happened (§2.4) and a fatigue draw takes no card, so neither counts | C #90; §2.4, R4, R55, R58, R225, R404 |
| R542 | Whose damage and whose deaths C #90 counts | Quest 5 ("your cards deal 12 damage to enemies") counts a hit whose source its controller controlled and whose target was the opponent's hero or a Unit the opponent controlled, both read as the hit landed. Quest 2 ("2 enemy permanents destroyed") counts a permanent the opponent controlled as it died, by anything, a stolen card included: the `destroyed` event names the controller a card died under (R172) | C #90; R404 |
| R543 | The order of C #90's Radiant rewards | The Radiant face's completed quest grants every reward it offers in the tree's order (A before B, C before D, …), then opens every quest they lead to. A reward that asks a question (B's target, D's placements, F's target, G's discards) still asks it; only the reward prompt itself is skipped | C #90; R404 |
| R545 | C #57 Echo copying an X-cost or embiggen Spell | Echo is played for its own printed (1) Cost, never for X or an embiggen price. When the text it has is an X-cost Spell's, the play chooses that X as a play's X is chosen (R81): at least 1, at most the player's current mana once Echo's price is paid (R348), so with nothing left after paying it cannot be played; a cast of it asks its caster for X as any cast X card's does. An embiggen Spell's text resolves at its base price | C #57; R65, R81, R348, R399 |
| R546 | C #57 Echo: which text runs, and when it is fixed | The copy is fixed as Echo's play begins (§10.5 step 1, or the start of a cast) and held until it lands: its declared choices, its resolution, its Echo repeats and its prompts' continuations run that Spell's face even if the play records a newer Spell; in hand and in a deck it follows the last Spell. The copied text is Echo's: "this" in it is Echo, its declared numbers are the copied definition's read on Echo's instance, and an Echo X the copied face prints adds to the Radiant face's Echo 1. Echo keeps its own name, (1) Cost, type and tags | C #57; R399 |
| R547 | C #57 Echo's static text | A copied Spell's Cast on draw casts an Echo as it is drawn (§2.4, R58), copying the Spell that is last as the cast begins, and the copied face's preview (R280) and yellow glow (R195) answer for Echo in hand. Text that acts from the graveyard after the play — §5.1's "End of turn: return this to your hand" (#23, #24, #31) — is not had: Echo lands at §10.5 step 7 and stays | C #57; §2.4, §5.1, R58, R155, R195, R280, R399 |
| R548 | C #66 EU Striker's timing | Both of C #66's triggers answer a play of its owner's once it has resolved (§10.5 step 7's `cardResolved`; a cast emits it, a countered play never does), as Hearthstone's "after you play" waits for resolution, and §8.6's row 66 reads "after it resolves" for both. R401 then holds by §10.3's dispatch, which offers an event to each card where it stands when it is dispatched: the `cardResolved` of the Unit that summons the Striker reaches it in hand, never on the field, and that of the card that bounces it reaches it on the field, never in hand | C #66; R70, R119, R401 |
| R549 | A cast on draw out of the opponent's deck repeats from your own | When a draw of yours takes a card from the opponent's deck (C #58 Common Resources, §6.3 Steal's cards between piles) and that card has Cast on draw, it is cast as yours (R58) and the draw it owes is drawn from the top of your own deck, as Hearthstone's "Casts When Drawn: draw a card" replaces the card with an ordinary draw of yours: the draw that took the cast card was yours, and only the effect that named the opponent's deck reached into it | C #58; §2.4, §6.3, R58 |
| R550 | C #63 Crop Dusting places on itself too | C #63's "each permanent" is every permanent on the field as it fires, the firing trap itself included: it takes its placement like any other, and its tokens go with it when it is spent to the graveyard as its firing ends (R78) | C #63; §6.3, R78 |
| R560 | C+ #26 Tommy Tempo with no zone | A Tommy Tempo cast on draw while its owner has no open, unlocked, unreserved unit zone is not cast: it goes to its owner's hand uncast (burned when the hand is full, R317), as R58's cap sends a card, and ends nothing. This follows the card's own text and is the exception to R138, under which a cast permanent with no zone still counts as played and resolves. A free 9/9 Taunt that ends your turn is worth casting only if it stands on the field; a cast that resolved with no body would take the turn for nothing. The engine's general rule for any Unit cast on draw (R459) gives the same result | C+ #26; §2.4, §6.2, R58, R70, R138, R317, R459 |
| R561 | C+ #31 Fusion Lab's Radiant ingredient (extends R77) | C+ #31's Radiant face fuses its random card in on that card's Radiant face, and that Radiant form is lent to both of the fusion's forms: the fused definition's base form and its Radiant form each join the ingredient's Radiant face to the kept hand card's matching face, so the rider shows whether or not the hand card is Radiant (a base hand card reads the ingredient's Radiant text), and the fused id marks that ingredient Radiant (R179). The kept card keeps its own radiant flag, its zone, its type and its cost (a costOverride of the cost it had, R65) | C+ #31; R77, R102, R179 |
| R562 | C+ #35 Rollback: too little history | "N turns ago" is the snapshot from the start of player-turn (this turn − N); when the history holds none that old it goes back to the oldest snapshot held (at most the start of turn 1, the empty board); `rolledBack` names how many turns it actually went back; with no history at all (a cast during setup) nothing happens and nothing is reported | C+ #35; R419 |
| R563 | C+ #35 Rollback: held zones | A zone held now on a restored side — a dying Reborn unit's (R64) or an animated card's home (R383) — is released, and it takes what the snapshot held there; a moved card's own home goes wherever it was; the snapshot's own homes come back for the cards standing there again; a Reborn unit whose zone a Rollback released does not return (as with a Lock, R47, R175) | C+ #35; R47, R64, R175, R383, R419 |
| R564 | C+ #29 Portal to the Past: a last board as the match keeps it | `createGame` keeps each seat's last board as a list of `{ defId, radiant }` in board order and nothing else, and drops, as the match is created, every entry the match could not rebuild from its id alone: an id its catalog lacks, a digest fused id (R468), whose list only the process that minted it can read, and a fused id (R179) any ingredient of which, all the way down, the catalog lacks. A seat left with no entry has no board, and a match with none hashes as one created without the input. C+ #29 picks among the different cards of its caster's board — each definition once, where it first appears, on its Radiant face if any of its entries was Radiant, never C+ #29 itself (R387) — so two copies of one card are one option, and Discover offers them all when there are fewer than 3. A fused entry's definition is rebuilt from its id into the match the first time it is offered or made, as R179 rebuilds its scripts: each ingredient as printed, priced as R65 reads it out of play | C+ #29; §9.3, §10.1, R65, R179, R387, R417, R468 |
| R565 | C+ #29 Portal to the Past: last boards on the server | The server keeps one last board per profile and kind (`public.last_boards`, migration 0017; kinds `server` and `practice`, only `server` written) and replaces it in the same transaction as each finished server match's result, however the match ended: each seat's board is the engine's `lastBoardFor` of the final state from that seat's side, so a face-down card the seat did not control never reaches its row. A match the reaper ends (R112) has no state to read, so it writes no board and the last one stays. Each match row freezes both seats' boards as it starts (`matches.p1_last_board`, `p2_last_board`, `[]` for older rows), so a rebuilt actor folds the same game whatever the profiles' rows say later (§9.3). No client role reads or writes either: RLS on with no policy and no grant, as `matches`; the server is the only reader and writer, and a profile's boards are deleted with it | C+ #29; §9.3, §9.5, R112, R417 |
| R566 | C+ #35 Rollback: what a put-back card keeps | It takes the snapshot's instance whole (definition, face, damage, buffs, keywords, position, counters, memory, Brittle, owner, face-up or face-down) except turn state: one that stood on the same side of the field just before and keeps its id keeps its present sickness and exertion (R171) and is reported only by `rolledBack`; any other entered on this turn — summoning sick, fresh exertion (R83, R171), a face-up trap re-set face-down on its own side included — and is named by `controlChanged`, with `formerId` when it took a fresh id (R227); a card mid-play in the resolving zone is not moved, and a carried Unit left with no carrier goes to its owner's hand | C+ #35; R73, R83, R171, R227, R419 |
| R569 | C+ #8 Withering Storm's random pick | C+ #8's "Degrade 4 random cards in your opponent's deck" draws its 4 different cards (R60) among the deck cards a Degrade can change now (§8.7), all of them when there are no more, drawing no random number when there are no more than 4 (R129). So that the number of `degraded` cues never tells either player how many deck cards a Degrade could change (R440), the pick is padded up to 4 (or the deck's size) with cards no change reaches, each cued with the change `none`. The Radiant face reaches every card of the deck once, as R440's sweep does | C+ #8; R60, R129, R386, R440 |
| R570 | C+ #10 New Wraps on a Unit that has Reborn | C+ #10's Reborn is a granted keyword (§10.4), recorded even on a Unit that already has Reborn: while the printed Reborn stands it changes nothing (keywords are a set, so the Unit still returns once, at 1 health, without Reborn), but a later Vanilla, which removes printed keywords and keeps granted ones, leaves it the granted Reborn | C+ #10; §10.4, R21, R78 |
| R572 | C+ #12.8 Frostspatula: a kill in its own last combat | "Every Unit this destroyed" counts a Unit whose death it caused in the same state-check pass as its own death (a mutual kill): its Death adds the Units this action's `destroyed` events name it the killer of (R42) to the ones it remembered, each once, so a 10/3 that trades with a 3/3 Rush Token still summons a fresh Rush Token for its controller | C+ #12.8; R42, R89, R409 |
| R578 | C+ #37 Wardrum: which play is the 3rd | Its controller's Spell, Field Spell and Trap plays of the turn (casts included, R70) are counted in the order they were played, a cast made inside a play's resolution coming after that play; Wardrum answers the resolution of the play whose place in that order is its threshold (3) and no other, so a 4th cast that resolves inside the 3rd's resolution does not set it off and the 3rd's own resolution does | C+ #37; R70, R464 |
| R580 | C+ #42 KY's Test's Easy problems | An Easy problem is generated from the match rng, never drawn from the bank: a + b with a and b each uniform from 10 to 99 (`KY_TEST_EASY_ADDENDS`), its three wrong options a + b moved by three different entries of ±1, ±2, ±10 (`KY_TEST_EASY_MISSES`, drawn as a shuffle of the six), so the four options always differ; the problem rides the answer prompt's resume data by its addends (`easy:<a>+<b>`), and the options are shown in an rng-shuffled order (R420, R465) | C+ #42; R420, R465 |
| R581 | C+ #40 Appropriations' Culture | Culture rolls every card of its caster's field (tops of piles and backrow cards), hand and deck, a Radiant one included, each its own roll of 10X% (Radiant 25X%, at most 100%), in field, hand, deck order — #42 Eugenics's reading (R177), not R60's pick among non-Radiant cards — so neither the draws nor the cues count the hidden Radiant cards; a success on a Radiant card changes nothing (cued when hidden), and a deck card made Radiant is listed as it went in (R311) | C+ #40; R60, R177, R311 |
| R582 | C+ #43 AI Slop at one card | AI Slop's declared `cards` may be Degraded to 1: a Fuse of one is no fusion (R77), so the one random AI generated card is added to the hand as it is, at `costOverride` 0 (Radiant on the Radiant face), the hand cap burning it | C+ #43; R77, R386 |
| R583 | C+ #44, #45 "highlight targets" | The Radiant face's preview (R280) has one value per choice its play declares, labelled "all permanents" and "only your opponent's": the count of permanents that choice would exile now, with their instance ids (`PreviewValue.ids`). An enemy face-down card is never named or counted, since its controller may not read its `loc` (R177), though the exile still takes it; the controller's own face-down cards are. The base face has no preview | C+ #44, C+ #45; R177, R280 |
| R584 | C+ #64 Mulch Muncher's discount prices a play | "Costs (1) less for each Fruit you've played this game" applies only where a play takes the card from: its player's hand, or a graveyard a permission lets them play it from (§6.3 Play, R454). It counts that player's Fruit-tagged plays this game (`playedByTag`, casts included, R70). Anywhere else (a deck, a graveyard with no permission, the field, a pool or a filter) it costs its printed (10), as Hearthstone's self-discounts work in hand only (R65). The cost floors at 0 | C+ #64; R55, R65, R70, R454 |
| R585 | C+ #70 Chaos Machine may pick itself | "A random card … on your side of the field" includes Chaos Machine itself, since the text does not say "other" (Hearthstone). Alone on its side with an empty hand, it Upgrades itself: its cost or its declared `cards`. The Upgrade is drawn and applied before the Degrade | C+ #70; R60, R386 |
| R586 | T-AI-3 Hallucination's copies and the deck's order | The copies reach the caster's hand in the order drawn, never the deck's, so the hand says nothing of where the originals lay (§9.1). A deck holding no more cards than it copies is copied whole with no draw (R129), in definition-id order. A copy the hand cap burns goes to the caster's graveyard and is given no Brittle | T-AI-3; §9.1, R57, R60, R129, R385 |
| R588 | T-AI-6 Datacenter Fire sweeps the backrow's Field Spells | "Destroy all Field Spells" takes the Field Spells acting in the backrow zones it names (the top of a backrow pile, and an Ivory Tower beneath the Unit it holds, R418); an Animated Field Spell standing in a unit zone is a Unit there (R383), so it is neither destroyed nor counted, and it is swept again once it is back in its backrow. Traps, Field Traps and Indestructible Field Spells are not destroyed and not counted | T-AI-6; R383, R418 |
| R589 | C+ #74 fuses only a card still on the field or in a graveyard | The card C+ #74 Twice Forward One Step Backwards fuses "if it still exists" is the played card on the field (either row) or in a graveyard as its play resolves. Exiled, returned to a hand or a deck, or ceased to exist, it is no card left to fuse: nothing is fused, and the even count still gives +1 Brittle, the trap staying face-down and unfired when it has fused nothing yet (R33) | C+ #74; R33, R385, R425 |
| R593 | A Call to Chaos a random cast makes is a link of its chain | A Call to Chaos (Core #95 or C+ #73) cast by a random cast — C+ #47 Jogg's Box, C+ #38.1 Solarius-Prime — is a cast of the R28 chain one link deeper than the card casting it, so its own recursion stops at `CALL_TO_CHAOS_CHAIN_CAP` casts in all; a later Call the same Box casts starts a chain of its own | C+ #47, C+ #38.1, #95, C+ #73; R28, R87, R452 |
| R594 | C+ #52's numbers are read as it resolves and carried | C+ #52 Jlockheed's Permanent Defense Contract reads its declared `cards` (and, Radiant, `discount`) through `param` as the Spell resolves and carries them in the rest-of-game effect's data, since each start-of-turn run re-enters with no card of its own (R127): a Degrade or an Upgrade made to the card before it was cast holds for the rest of the game, and nothing done to a later copy changes an effect already in force | C+ #52; R127, R386, R458 |
| R596 | "It" after a draw is the card that draw put in the hand | A text that draws and then acts on "it" or "the drawn card" — prices it (C+ #65.2 Normal Grape's "It costs (1) less", C+ #65.3 Large Grape's "It costs (0)") or reads it (T-AI-4 Chain of Thought's "If it costs (1) or less, repeat this") — means the card that one draw itself put in its player's hand. A card cast on draw never reaches the hand (R58), and the card the cast-on-draw chain's repeat then brings is that repeat's draw, not this one's; a burned card is in the graveyard (R4), a fatigue draw brings none (§2.4), a draw a limit stops is no draw (R457): each of these leaves "it" nothing, so nothing is priced and Chain of Thought's chain ends. Hearthstone's reading of "draw a card; it costs less" | C+ #65.2, C+ #65.3, T-AI-4; §2.4, R4, R58, R457 |
| R600 | Who stays on the shadow-ban watch list | R390's `SHADOW_WATCH` holds the cards a sweep found at risk by their own numbers and did not ban: some tier's pass-1 or pass-2 numbers meet a flag's condition at half strength (affordable on `minAffordableTurns` turns and played at most once, or a mean evaluation change below half of `selfHarmDelta`). A card that was at risk only because it stood on the old ban or the old watch list, and whose numbers in both passes are clean this time, comes off: it is no longer on track to be banned. So a card cleared once stays watched for one more sweep, and the list does not only grow. Each entry names the numbers that put it there, per tier and pass | §9.9, R186, R390 |
| R601 | The numbers a judgement ban reads | A `neverPlayed` or `selfHarm` ban reads pass 2's numbers at one tier and nothing else: the card's games of that tier in pass 2, the ones it was forced into and the ones it was dealt as at-risk filler, summed. `neverPlayed` needs `banAffordableTurns` (6) affordable turns and no play in any of them; `selfHarm` needs `banHarmPlays` (8) plays whose mean evaluation change is below `selfHarmDelta`. Pass 1's numbers are never added in: they only put a card at risk, so a card at risk that pass 2 did not sweep is not banned for either. `error` and `timeout` count the card's own forced games of both passes, as R186 always did | §9.9, R186, R390 |
| R602 | The AI sees what a hidden card visibly does | R403 makes a face-down Trap or Field Trap with no activation condition live, so a hidden card can change what both players see: C #88 Siphon Squad's aura shrinks the enemy's Attack while it stays face-down. R185's redaction turns the card into a placeholder, and without more the AI's determinizations would hand its units their Attack back and it would declare attacks the engine refuses. So the redaction keeps every unit's Attack and Health as the board shows them (the difference the placeholder made goes on the unit's buffs), while the card itself stays hidden. The AI learns nothing a player does not read off the board, and a determinization agrees with the board it was dealt from: a hidden backrow card is never sampled as a Trap or Field Trap whose live aura would change a unit's shown Attack, Health or keywords | §9.9, R33, R185, R403; C #88 |
| R603 | Hidden Glicko-2 rating | Every player carries a hidden Glicko-2 rating — rating, deviation and volatility — starting at `RATING_START` with a new player's deviation (`RATING_DEVIATION_START`) and volatility (`RATING_VOLATILITY_START`). One rated game is one rating period for both sides, each rated against the other's rating from before the game, so neither side's update depends on which is computed first. Draws, offered or by the turn cap, score 0.5 for both; concedes and disconnects count as losses like any other ending. The update is deterministic: the same inputs give the same outputs on every machine, checked against Glickman's worked example and independently computed reference values, draws included | §9.12, R79 |
| R604 | Only the ranked match type rates | Only a match the queue paired — `ranked` on the match row — moves a rating or a rank. A room challenge is unranked: its row records both ratings unchanged, and no season row is written. A game of a Conquest series is recorded but not rated; a ranked series moves the rating once, rated as one game between its two sides when it ends, from the ratings at the time it ends, and an abandoned series moves nothing | §9.5, §9.12, R262, R263 |
| R605 | Grape tiers | A player who has not finished the season's placement games (`RANK_PLACEMENT_GAMES`) is shown as Raisin, with how many placements they have played. Past them, the ladder is one integer per player — pips above the bottom of Rotten Grape III — read as Rotten Grape, Normal Grape, Large Grape, Golden Grape and Mythic Grape, each of three divisions (`RANK_DIVISIONS_PER_TIER`, III at the bottom, I at the top) of `RANK_PIPS_PER_DIVISION` pips. A rating's percentile among the season's placed players, read off `RANK_TIER_PERCENTS` (Rotten 12, Normal 60, Large 20, Golden 7, Mythic 1), is the rank it calls for; the percents are config values, tuned without code changes. The game that completes a player's placements puts them straight at the rank their rating calls for | §9.12 |
| R606 | Gentle convergence and win-streak bonus | A win gives `RANK_WIN_PIPS` pips and a loss takes `RANK_LOSS_PIPS`; a draw moves nothing. A game played a division or more (`RANK_CONVERGENCE_GAP_PIPS`) away from the rank the player's new rating calls for leans `RANK_CONVERGENCE_PIPS` pip harder toward it when the game's own move already heads that way — a win below the called-for rank gives one pip more, a loss above it takes one pip more — so the visible rank converges on true skill without big point swings; a game heading away moves its usual pip. A win streak of `RANK_STREAK_LENGTH` or more earns `RANK_STREAK_BONUS_PIPS` bonus pips per win below Mythic Grape; a loss ends the streak and a draw neither extends nor ends it | §9.12 |
| R607 | Tier floors and season peaks | A player never drops below the Grape tier they have reached this season — the floor rises with them — though divisions inside a tier can still drop, and wins past the top hold it. The season keeps each player's best: the highest ladder reached and the best (lowest) Jlorious position held. The season's badge shows on the profile: the Jlorious position, or the Grape tier and division of the highest ladder; a season whose placements were never finished has none | §9.12 |
| R608 | Jlorious is the top 100 Mythic players by rating | Jlorious is not a ladder position but the season's placed Mythic Grape players by hidden rating, highest first, ties broken by profile id: the first `JLORIOUS_SIZE` (100) of them, shown as a numbered leaderboard position (#1–#100) instead of divisions. When fewer than 100 Mythic players qualify, every one of them is Jlorious. A player who falls out of the top 100 is Mythic Grape again, where the tier floor holds them | §9.12 |
| R609 | Seasons on the minor version, opened with a soft reset | A season is named by the minor version of the game (`v<major>.<minor>` of the newest patch): every patch of v0.2 plays in season `v0.2`, and the first patch of v0.3 opens the next. Opening a season past the first runs the soft reset over every rated player in the same transaction as the season row: each rating pulled `SEASON_RESET_STRENGTH` of the way toward the players' mean, each deviation widened by `SEASON_RESET_DEVIATION_BOOST` in quadrature and capped at a new player's, volatility kept. Everyone starts the season back in Raisin placements; bots are not reset. The reset runs the same path against a copy of real data first (`--dry-run`, which reports and rolls back) before going live | §9.12 |
| R610 | Each AI bot keeps its own rating | Each AI bot keeps its own internally maintained Glicko-2 rating, starting at a new player's values, so a rated game that seats a bot rates it by the same rule as a player and records its side with the `ai` pilot and its bot id. A bot has no ladder rank, no season row and no place on the player leaderboard. No live mode seats a bot today — practice runs in the player's browser and records no result (R187) — and a rated game against the AI, when a mode produces one, counts like any other | §9.12 |
| R611 | Every rated game is recorded | Every rated game is recorded under its id — a match's, or a series' — once; rating the same id again changes nothing. The record carries the season, the patch and build version, the catalog version, each side's pilot (`human`, or `ai` with the bot id), the result (the winning side, or a draw) and the reason, and both sides' hidden ratings and visible ranks before and after | §9.5, §9.12 |
| R612 | Reads never show the hidden rating | The client reads its own season, tag, rank, streak, record and season badges (`GET /api/ranked`), the leaderboard (`GET /api/leaderboard`: Jlorious #1–#100, then every other placed player grouped by Grape tier, highest first, then how many are still placing), and both seats' ranks for the match screen. The profile shows its tier, division and pips with its badges. No read carries the hidden rating, deviation or volatility — not even to its owner — and a caller learns nothing about a match it is not in | §9.1, §9.12 |
| R630 | The Card Almanac | `/almanac` is public: it renders signed out, with no redirect to sign-in, and calls no endpoint (not the catalog's, the collection's, the decks' or the account's), reading the catalog the bundle ships. It shows every catalog card, tokens included, since L3's reason for leaving tokens out of the deck builder's pool is a deck's and the almanac builds none; with no filter, cost ascending, base faces. Its filter bar, card grid, empty state and detail view are the deck builder's own components, through one browse pane both screens render, so the two cannot drift apart: the filter bar without its "owned only" control and with AI and Token after the deck builder's tag chips, the grid read-only (no "+", no drag, no deck or ownership marks; a card's name is its name, type, cost and rarity), and the detail view with no action but Close and a mic dropdown previewing the card's voice lines, its line under the glossary the rarity, and for a token its printed rarity if any, then "Token · not deckable". The deck builder otherwise renders exactly as before. Linked from the site footer beside Patch notes, listed in the sitemap, and served by vercel.json. Presentation only (CLAUDE.md rule 7) | §2.6, §5.1, §9.4, §10.10, R251, R388, R503 |
