| R631 | Music | The client plays music on its own bus, under the master volume and mute. Every screen without a board plays the main menu theme. On a board, each viewer hears music driven by their own view (R203), by a priority stack: the result first (a victory, defeat or draw sting and its loop), then a Mythic card's theme or the shared Legendary entrance theme, then the station's low-health track (the viewer's hero at or under a quarter of HERO_HEALTH, rounded down), then the station's in-game track. While the opponent has the turn, the in-game track plays through a low-pass at a lower level; the mulligan is nobody's turn. A match opens on its station's sting into one of the station's in-game tracks, rotated per device from match to match, unless its first view is past the first turn (a reconnect). A theme or a station switch starts at the moment R204 gives the card's cast line, and only for a card the viewer can read: a Trap's at its firing, never at its set. A Mythic's theme holds until the viewer's hero is next hit, another Mythic replaces it, or the game ends. A Legendary's shared entrance theme holds the same way, except that another Legendary replaces it with its own shared track. Away from low health a theme also ends once it has played twice. The same card cast again does not restart its theme. A card that switches its caster's station does so for the rest of the match, and the next match starts on the station the settings name. A hotseat hand-over plays the arriving seat's own music, and no theme carries over. A change waits for the playing track's next bar line (at most 3 s) and crossfades over 1.5 s. A track that opens on a sting comes in at once, and a station's tracks pick up within a match where they left off. With dynamic music off, only the station's in-game track plays. When the page is hidden or the window loses focus the music fades out, and fades back in on return, unless the player turned on "Keep playing music when this tab is in the background" (off by default; effects and voice lines stay quiet in a hidden tab either way) | Every Mythic (#96–#100, C #90, C+ #27, C+ #74); every non-token Legendary shares one of two entrance themes (`legendary-1` for the Core and Classic Legendaries, `legendary-2` for the Classic+ ones); the station switches #4 (Tavern), #50 and C #5 (EDM), C+ #39 and #23 (Lo-fi), C+ #37 and C+ #8 (Epic Orchestral) |
| R632 | A session belongs to its tab | The access token and its renewal are kept in the tab's `sessionStorage`, never in `localStorage`, so a script that reads the shared storage finds no token and a token does not outlive its tab on a shared computer. A reload keeps the session; a new tab, or a browser that was closed, asks the player to sign in again, and a duplicated tab starts with a copy of its original's. A session an older build left in `localStorage` moves into the first tab that reads it and is deleted there, and nothing writes that key again: every sign-in and renewal also deletes any copy left. A tab's own session is preferred to the shared storage's. Signing out clears the tab's session, any copy in `localStorage` and the end-to-end fixtures' session (`localStorage["jackioh.e2e.session"]`, e2e/README.md A6, read only when the tab has no session of its own), and revokes this tab's session at the provider (R194); a tab signed in separately keeps its own. Reading never throws on blocked storage: with none, the player is signed out at the next page load. The privacy policy says where the session is kept | §9.2, R193, R194 |
| R633 | Game settings kept on the account | An active account keeps its game settings on the server as well as on the device, so a player who signs in on another device, or clears site data, finds them there: one `player_settings` row per profile, holding the settings in groups (the client has four: `gameplay`, `audio`, `fx` and `cards`, one per store of the settings dialog), each `{ at, values }` with the time it last changed on the writing device and a flat object of booleans, numbers and short texts. A player reads only its own row and writes none of it directly (RLS, §9.1). The server writes it through `GET` and `PUT /api/settings`, which are `active` routes (a pending account has only the code screen, §9.4) and take the profile from the verified token, never from the body. The body is checked first: at most `PLAYER_SETTINGS_GROUPS_MAX` (8) groups, each a lower-case slug of at most `PLAYER_SETTINGS_NAME_MAX_LENGTH` (40) characters holding at most `PLAYER_SETTINGS_KEYS_MAX` (32) settings named by letters and digits of the same length, each a boolean, a finite number or a text of at most `PLAYER_SETTINGS_TEXT_MAX_LENGTH` (40) characters, and a whole-millisecond time; and the groups an account holds come to at most `PLAYER_SETTINGS_BYTES_MAX` (4096) bytes, a write past either cap answered 409 with nothing written. The server does not know the settings, which are the client's: a group or a setting it is sent is kept, and a client that does not have it ignores it. None of it is a rule: nothing the engine does reads a setting (CLAUDE.md rule 7) | §9.1, §9.4, §10.11, R320 |
| R634 | Merging the device's settings with the account's | A write names the groups a device has changed, and a group replaces the stored one only when its time is strictly later: a stale device can never undo a newer change, a tie changes nothing (so the same write twice is the same write once), a group a write does not name stays, and a change to one group never undoes another's. A time after the server's clock counts as now, so a device whose clock runs ahead cannot pin its settings. The merge runs under a lock on the profile, so two devices writing at once cannot lose each other's group. The client keeps, per group, when the device last changed it, and on a screen with an active account reads the account's copy once and takes the newer side of each group: a group the account holds with a later time than the device's, or one the device has never changed, is applied to the device's store (which keeps what it knows and clamps the rest); a group the device changed later than the account holds is sent up. After that each change on the device (a switch, a slider, a reset, another tab's change) is sent a moment later, so a dragged slider is one request, one request at a time, the answer reconciled again; a group the server kept, with this device's values, at an earlier time than the device's is held at the server's time, so a clock running ahead does not resend it for ever. The device's copy is the one the game reads and is authoritative for the session: a request that fails or never answers changes nothing on screen and raises nothing, and the dialog's Account tab says so and offers Try again, while a change, coming back online or the next load sends what is owed. Signed out, pending or banned, settings stay on the device and nothing is sent. Settings belong to the device as much as to the account, so signing in on a device adds its later changes to the account | §9.1, §9.4, §10.11, R320, R321, R633 |
| R635 | Cast on draw cards sit out setup | A card that casts on draw (§2.4: the printed keyword, the `castOnDraw` enchantment, or a copied Spell's text) is never dealt by §2.1's opening draw or by R9's replacement draws, so nothing is cast before turn 1 and a mulligan chooses between cards that are only what they look like. The cards stay in their owner's library through the deal and the mulligans, behind every card the draws can reach, so the library count says nothing of them (§9.1, as R225). Once both mulligans have resolved, each seat's are shuffled into its library, each at a uniformly random place by the match rng, before The Coin (R244) and the start-of-game effects (§2.1 step 4): they wait at the bottom of the library, so left where they lay they would be the last cards drawn, and they are taken out and put back one at a time instead. Nothing is reported for the shuffle-in: a `shuffledIn` per card would count them for the other seat (R97). A Quickdraw card that also casts on draw is a Quickdraw card first: dealt as the draw it replaces (R225) and never cast. A seat's setup draws are capped at the cards it may draw, so a deck with few cards besides cast-on-draw ones deals a short hand and one with none deals an empty hand, which still has its (empty) mulligan to answer, and a mulligan that returns more cards than the library holds besides them is dealt fewer back. Setup never deals fatigue (R3): the first draw that meets a library of nothing but cast-on-draw cards is turn 1's, a chain bounded by R58's cap like any other. What can still ask a question in setup is a start-of-game clause as its card arrives in a hand (R151), which R224 holds setup for. Not reachable with a legal deck (a deck holds one copy of a card, L3, so at most five non-token cards cast on draw), but `createGame` takes any deck | §2.1, §2.4, R3, R9, R58, R151, R224, R225, R244, R640, #21, #27 |
| R636 | Windfury | A Unit with Windfury, read through the layers (§10.4), may declare `WINDFURY_ATTACKS` (2) attacks each turn, where every other Unit declares one (§4.1). Each is a full attack: it is validated by §4.2's steps 1 to 3 and opens its own trap window, and a Unit that is dead, no longer in Attack Position or no longer legal to attack with is refused the second as it would be the first. Declaring the first spends the exertion a position switch needs, so the Unit cannot switch afterwards, and a Unit that switched cannot attack (R6); Deft Duelist (R49) keeps its independent switch. The count is read when the second attack is declared, not when the first was: a Unit that gains Windfury after one attack may attack once more, and one that loses it after the first may not attack again. `legalActions` and the reducer's refusal ask the same function (`combat.hasExertion`), so they agree on the second attack. An attack an effect forces (R53, R20) spends no exertion and is not counted. Windfury is a Unit keyword in the random keyword pool (R21), so Plastic Surgery, Zao Gao and an Upgrade can reach it; no card prints it yet | §4.1, §6.1; R6, R20, R21, R49, R53 |
| R637 | Temporary | Temporary is a card keyword: a card is Temporary while the set of keywords of a card in a hand (printed on its running face, or granted) holds it. At the end of its owner's turn, in cleanup after every end-of-turn trigger, trap and delayed effect (R62), each Temporary card in their hand is discarded, in hand order, with no choice and no rng draw; it is a discard (§6.3), so "whenever you discard" sees each one and a unit-token card ceases to exist (R11). A Temporary card in the other player's hand waits for that player's own turn end. It does nothing on the field, in a deck or in a graveyard; R215 takes a granted Temporary off a card that reaches a graveyard. A Vanilla card loses a printed Temporary and keeps a given one (§10.4). The keyword is not in R21's random pool, and it is not temporary mana (§2.3): the engine names the card keyword `Temporary` and the mana `gainMana`, so the two cannot be confused. C+ #54 Book of Books gives each Book it adds, in both its faces, a granted Temporary once the Book is in the hand (a Book the full hand burns carries none); the card's printed text names it in the next card patch | C+ #54; §2.2, §6.1, §6.3, R11, R62, R215 |
| R638 | Brittle ticks on the field only | A Brittle count is kept on the card in every zone (R78, R385) but ticks only while the card is on the field, in R68's order at the start of its controller's turn (units by lane, the top of each Stack pile only, then the backrow). A card in a hand or a deck holds its count: it does not drop, nothing crumbles there and no event reports it, however long it waits. A count a card holds off the field starts its turn cycle as the card enters the field from a hand, a deck, a graveyard or the resolving zone, so its first tick is the first start of its controller's turn numbered t + 2 or later of the arrival (`BRITTLE_FIRST_TICK_TURNS`, R385), however long it was held; a move from one field zone to another (a steal, a swap, a rotation, an Animated step) is no arrival and starts nothing. A card that leaves the field keeps the count it has, held until it is back. R13 and R441 stand: a card dormant under a Stack is not on the field, so it does not tick, and a spent count (0) is dropped by R78's and R215's reset. A Brittle crumble is a destroy on the field, so `crumbled` always reports `zone: "field"` | C+ #23, C+ #74, T-AI-3; §6.1, R13, R78, R385, R441 |
| R639 | Player statistics and the homescreen's rotation | The device keeps its player's tally (§9.11): games finished, won, lost and drawn, and per card the games it was seen in, the times the player played it, the times the opponent played it where the player could read it, the times a copy of the player's own was destroyed and the times one of the opponent's was. A game counts when its view says it is over, once, from the viewer's seat; online matches and practice games count, a tutorial lesson and a hotseat game do not. It is read off the `PlayerView`s the board is handed and the events new with each (the view's window slides, so only what the last view lacked is counted), so a card the view hides (the sentinel, a face-down trap, the opponent's hand, the viewer's own library) is never named. It lives in `localStorage` and nowhere else, tolerates a missing or hand-edited value, and is clearable. Once the device has logged `ROTATION_MIN_GAMES` (10) games the homescreen's hand is dealt from every shipped set's non-token cards, weighted so a card that prints at full size is `FEATURE_WEIGHT_PLAIN` (4) times as likely as one that needs shrinking (never excluded), and swaps one slot every `ROTATION_INTERVAL_MS` (7 s) for a card of the same rarity it is not showing; below the threshold R374's Core deal stands. The hand holds still under reduced motion, under a pointer or focus, with a card open and while the page is hidden. A face opens its card's detail view on a click, a tap, Enter or Space. The weighting draws from the page's injected random source like R374's deal. Presentation and bookkeeping only (CLAUDE.md rule 7) | §9.1, §9.11, §10.10, R97, R374, R376 |
| R640 | A Quickdraw card replaces an opening draw | §2.1's "any Quickdraw card in a deck replaces one of these draws" is read literally (R225): a card cannot replace a draw that does not exist, so a seat is dealt at most as many Quickdraw cards as its opening hand holds (§2.1's table entry plus its handicap's extra cards, R182), the first ones in the shuffle's order, and the others stay in the library as ordinary cards, drawn later like any other card. So the opening hand is always exactly that size, whatever the deck holds, and the other seat can never count a seat's Quickdraw cards from it (§9.1, R225), and a deal never overruns the hand cap (R4). A deck holds one copy of a card (L3), so five Quickdraw cards is the most it can hold (#65, #84, #98, C #90, C+ #37): the first seat's three draws take three of them, the second seat's four take four, and all five are dealt only to a seat with five draws, a Medium or Hard AI going second. The source's "add this to your starting hand" and BUILD M1-T5's "puts both in the opening hand" were silent on more Quickdraw cards than draws; the other reading, every Quickdraw card entering the hand with the random draws floored at 0, would have given a deck of five a hand larger than the table's. Issue #152 | §2.1, §6.2, R4, R182, R225, R635, #65, #84, #98, C #90, C+ #37 |
| R650 | A micro patch's version | An issue titled `Patch vA.B.Y: …` is a micro patch: the patch command names it after the newest version in the patch list (its last entry, R388), with the next letter — after v0.2.5 it is v0.2.5b, after v0.2.7c it is v0.2.7d — and refuses when that version is not an A.B one or has no vA.B.C form. This is the only place a version string is read, and only when a patch is made; everywhere else R105 holds (equality only, never parsed or ordered). `vA.B.X` is the designer's to replace before the patch is made | R105, R388; docs/issues-and-patches.md |
